-non-commutative probability space containing a family of pairs of
B
-algebras
\{(C_{k},D_{k})\}_{k\in K}
. Consider the following two conditions:

(1)

The family
\{(C_{k},D_{k})\}_{k\in K}
is bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
.

(2)

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n}
,
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in A
, and non-constant maps
\gamma:\{1,\ldots,n\}\to K
such that

Z_{k}\in\begin{cases}C_{\gamma(k)}&\text{if }\chi(k)=\ell\\
D_{\gamma(k)}&\text{if }\chi(k)=r\\
\end{cases},

it follows that

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})=0.

Then (1) implies (2). In the case that
\tau_{B}:B\to\mathbb{C}
is faithful, (2) implies (1).

Proof.

Note (1) implies (2) follows from
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 8.1.1 and Theorem
4.13
. In the case that
\tau_{B}
is faithful, (2) immediately implies
\kappa^{B}_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n})=0
where
\{Z_{k}\}^{n}_{k=1}
are as in (2) via Theorem
4.13
. Hence
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 8.1.1 completes the argument.
∎

Vanishing Analytical Cumulants

However, something stronger than Corollary
4.14
holds. Indeed, note that the analytic operator-valued bi-free cumulant function has the added benefit that the last entry can be an element of
L_{2}(A,\tau)
and thus the
L_{2}
-image of a product of left and right operators. As such, it is possible to verify that additional analytic bi-cumulants vanish.

The desired result is analogous to the scalar-valued result demonstrated in
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 2.30 and proved in a similar manner. Thus we begin with a generalization of
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 9.1.5 where we can expand out a cumulant involving products of operators. In
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 9.1.5 only products of left and right operators were considered in the operator-valued setting whereas
[
CS2020
]
*Lemma 2.29 expanded out scalar-valued cumulants involving a product of left and right operator in the last entry.

To begin, fix
m<n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\chi\in{\{\ell,r\}}^{m}
, integers

k(0)=0<k(1)<\ldots<k(m)=n,

and any function
\widehat{\chi}\in{\{\ell,r\}}^{n}
such that for all
q\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
for which there exists a (necessarily unique)
p_{q}\in\{1,\ldots,m-1\}
with
k(p_{q}-1)<q\leq k(p_{q})
, we have

\widehat{\chi}(q)=\chi(p_{q}).

Thus
\widehat{\chi}
is constant from
k(p-1)+1
to
k(p)
whereas
\widehat{\chi}
does not need to be constant from
k(m-1)+1
to
k(m)
.

We may embed
\BNC(\chi)
into
\BNC(\widehat{\chi})
via
\pi\mapsto\widehat{\pi}
where the blocks of
\widehat{\pi}
are formed by taking each block
V
of
\pi
and forming a block

\widehat{V}=\bigcup_{p\in V}\{k(p-1)+1,\ldots,k(p)\}

of
\widehat{\pi}
. It is not difficult to see that
\widehat{\pi}\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})
as
\widehat{\chi}
is constant on
\{k(p-1)+1,\ldots,k(p)\}
for all
p\in V\setminus\{m\}
and although the block containing
\{k(n-1)+1,\ldots,k(n)\}
has both left and right entries, it occurs at the bottom of the bi-non-crossing diagram and thus poses no problem. Alternatively, this map can be viewed as an analogue of the map on non-crossing partitions from
[
NS2006
]
*Notation 11.9 after applying
s^{-1}_{\chi}
.

It is easy to see that
\widehat{1_{\chi}}=1_{\widehat{\chi}}
,

\widehat{0_{\chi}}=\bigcup_{p=1}^{m}\{k(p-1)+1,\ldots,k(p)\},

and that the map
\pi\mapsto\widehat{\pi}
is injective and order-preserving. Furthermore, the image of
\BNC(\chi)
under this map is

\widehat{\BNC}(\chi)=\left[\widehat{0_{\chi}},\widehat{1_{\chi}}\right]=\left[\widehat{0_{\chi}},1_{\widehat{\chi}}\right]\subseteq\BNC(\widehat{\chi}).

Remark 4.15
.

Recall that since
\mu_{\BNC}
is the bi-non-crossing Möbius function, we have for each
\sigma,\pi\in\BNC(\chi)
with
\sigma\leq\pi
that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\upsilon\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\sigma\leq\upsilon\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\BNC}(\upsilon,\pi)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{if }\sigma=\pi\\
0&\mbox{otherwise }\end{array}\right..

Since the lattice structure is preserved under the map defined above, we see that
\mu_{\BNC}(\sigma,\pi)=\mu_{\BNC}(\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\pi})
.

It is also easy to see that the partial Möbius inversion from
[
NS2006
]
*Proposition 10.11 holds in the bi-free setting; that is, if
f,g:\BNC(\chi)\to\mathbb{C}
are such that

f(\pi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\sigma\leq\pi\end{subarray}}g(\sigma)

for all
\pi\in\BNC(\chi)
, then for all
\pi,\sigma\in\BNC(\chi)
with
\sigma\leq\pi
, we have the relation

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\upsilon\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\sigma\leq\upsilon\leq\pi\end{subarray}}f(\upsilon)\mu_{\BNC}(\upsilon,\pi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\omega\vee\sigma=\pi\end{subarray}}g(\omega)

where
\pi\vee\sigma
denotes the smallest element of
\BNC(\chi)
greater than
\pi
and
\sigma
.

Thus, by following the proofs of either
[
NS2006
]
*Theorem 11.12,
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 9.1.5, or
[
CS2020
]
*Lemma 2.29, we arrive at the following.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space. Under the above notation, if
\pi\in\BNC(\chi)
and
Z_{k}\in A_{\widehat{\chi}(k)}
, then

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{k(1)},\ldots,Z_{k(m-2)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m-1)},Z_{k(m-1)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m)}+N_{\tau})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=\widehat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau}).

In particular, when
\sigma=1_{\chi}
, we have

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{k(1)},\ldots,Z_{k(m-2)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m-1)},Z_{k(m-1)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m)}+N_{\tau})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=1_{\widehat{\chi}}\\
\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau}),

Proof.

First, it is not difficult to verify using the recursive definition of the analytic operator-valued bi-moment function that

\Psi_{\upsilon}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{k(1)},\ldots,Z_{k(m-2)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m-1)},Z_{k(m-1)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m)}+N_{\tau})=\Psi_{\widehat{\upsilon}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})

for all
\upsilon\in\BNC(\chi)
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{k(1)},\ldots,Z_{k(m-2)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m-1)},Z_{k(m-1)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m)}+N_{\tau})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\upsilon\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\upsilon\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\Psi_{\upsilon}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{k(1)},\ldots,Z_{k(m-2)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m-1)},Z_{k(m-1)+1}\cdots Z_{k(m)}+N_{\tau})\mu_{\BNC}(\upsilon,\pi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\upsilon\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\upsilon\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\Psi_{\widehat{\upsilon}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})\mu_{\BNC}(\widehat{\upsilon},\widehat{\pi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\widehat{0_{\chi}}\leq\sigma\leq\widehat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\Psi_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})\mu_{\BNC}(\sigma,\widehat{\pi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=\widehat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})

where the last line following from Remark
4.15
.
∎

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space containing a family of pairs of
B
-algebras
\{(C_{k},D_{k})\}_{k\in K}
that are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
. For each
k\in K
, let
L_{2}(A_{k},\tau)
be the closed subspace of
L_{2}(A,\tau)
generated by

\alg(C_{k},D_{k})+N_{\tau}.

Then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n}
, non-constant maps
\gamma:\{1,\ldots,n\}\to K
,
\xi\in L_{2}(A_{\gamma(n)},\tau)
, and
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1}\in A
such that

Z_{k}\in\begin{cases}C_{\gamma(k)}&\text{if }\chi(k)=\ell\\
D_{\gamma(k)}&\text{if }\chi(k)=r\\
\end{cases},

it follows that

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)=0.

Proof.

Fix an
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{m}
, non-constant map
\gamma:\{1,\ldots,m\}\to K
, and
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m-1}\in A
. For any
n\geq m
, if
Z_{m},\ldots,Z_{n}\in C_{\gamma(m)}\cup D_{\gamma(m)}
and
\widehat{\chi}
is defined by

\widehat{\chi}(k)=\begin{cases}\chi(k)&\text{if }k\leq m\\
\ell&\text{if }k>m\text{ and }Z_{k}\in C_{\gamma(m)}\\
r&\text{if }k>m\text{ and }Z_{k}\in D_{\gamma(m)}\\
\end{cases},

then by Proposition
4.16
and Theorem
4.8
implies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m-1},Z_{m}\cdots Z_{n}+N_{\tau})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=1_{\widehat{\chi}}\\
\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n}+N_{\tau})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})\\
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=1_{\widehat{\chi}}\\
\end{subarray}}\kappa^{B}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n})+N_{\tau_{B}}.

As the conditions
\sigma\in\BNC(\widehat{\chi})
and
\sigma\vee\widehat{0_{\chi}}=1_{\widehat{\chi}}
automatically imply that each block of
\sigma
containing one of
\{1,2,\ldots,m-1\}
must also contain an element of
\{m,\ldots,n\}
, the bi-multiplicative properties of the operator-valued bi-free cumulant function imply that each cumulant
\kappa^{B}_{\sigma}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},Z_{n})
appearing in the sum above can be reduced down to an expression involving a mixed
\kappa^{B}
term which must be 0 by
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Theorem 8.1.1. Hence

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m-1},Z_{m}\cdots Z_{n}+N_{\tau})=0.

Since
\widetilde{E}
is a continuous function and left multiplication of
A
on
L_{2}(A,\tau)
yields bounded operators, due to the recursive nature of
\Psi
we see that
\Psi
is continuous in the
L_{2}(A,\tau)
entry. Therefore, by Möbius inversion,
\widetilde{\kappa}
is continuous in the
L_{2}(A,\tau)
entry. Hence the result follows.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space and let
n\geq 2
,
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n}
,
\xi\in L_{2}(A,\tau)
,
b\in B
, and
Z_{k}\in A_{\chi(k)}
. Suppose that either there exists
p\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
such that

Z_{p}=\begin{cases}L_{b}&\text{if }\chi(p)=\ell\\
R_{b}&\text{if }\chi(p)=r\end{cases}

or that
\xi\in L_{2}(B,\tau_{B})
. Then

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)=0.

Proof.

If
Z_{p}=L_{b}
or
Z_{p}=R_{b}
for some
p
, then we may proceed as in the proof of Theorem
4.17
by assuming that
\xi
is an element of
A
, expanding out the analytic operator-valued bi-free cumulant function with the aid of Proposition
4.16
, and using the fact that non-singleton operator-valued bi-free cumulants involving
L_{b}
or
R_{b}
terms are zero by
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Proposition 6.4.1 and then taking a limit at the end.

In the case where
\xi\in L_{2}(B,\tau_{B})
,
\xi
is a limit of terms of the form
L_{b}+N_{\tau}
. As

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},L_{b}+N_{\tau})=\kappa_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},L_{b})+N_{\tau_{B}}=0+N_{\tau_{B}},

by Theorem
4.13
and
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Proposition 6.4.1 the result follows by taking a limit.
∎

5.
Bi-Free Conjugate Variables with respect to Completely Positive Maps

In this section we develop the appropriate notion of conjugate variables in order to define bi-free Fisher information and entropy with respect to completely positive maps. This can be viewed as both an extension of the bi-free conjugate variables developed in
[
CS2020
]
and of the free conjugate variables with respect to a completely positive map developed in
[
S2000
]
. We will focus on both the moment and cumulant characterizations of these conjugate variables, whereas
[
S2000
]
focused on the moment and derivation characterizations of free conjugate variables. Although
[
CS2020
]
analyzed the moment, cumulant, and bi-free difference quotient characterizations of bi-free conjugate variables, we will forgo trying to generalize the bi-free difference quotient characterization in this setting as it was the cumulant characterization that was found most effective and as the bi-module structures of
[
S2000
]
necessary for the derivation characterization using adjoints are less clear in this setting.

We refer the reader to
[
NSS1999
]
*Definition 2.7 as an equivalent description to
[
S2000
]
of the free conjugate variables with respect to a completely positive map that we mimic below.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, let
Y\in A_{r}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map.

An element
\xi\in L_{2}(A,\tau)
is said to satisfy the
left bi-free conjugate variable relations for
X
with respect to
\eta
and
\tau
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
if for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\cup C_{r}
we have

\tau(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{n}\xi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tau\left(\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n+1\}}Z_{p}\right)L_{\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)}\right),

where
V_{k}=\{k<m<n+1\,\mid\,Z_{m}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\}
and where all products are taken in numeric order (with the empty product being 1). If, in addition,

\xi\in\overline{\alg(X,C_{\ell},C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

we call
\xi
the
left bi-free conjugate variable for
X
with respect to
\eta
and
\tau
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
and denote
\xi
by
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
.

Similarly, an element
\nu\in L_{2}(A,\tau)
is said to satisfy the
right bi-free conjugate variable relations for
Y
with respect to
\eta
and
\tau
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
if for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\{Y\}\cup C_{\ell}\cup C_{r}
we have

\tau(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{n}\nu)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tau\left(\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n+1\}}Z_{p}\right)R_{\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)}\right),

where
V_{k}=\{k<m<n+1\,\mid\,Z_{m}\in\{Y\}\cup C_{r}\}
. If, in addition,

\nu\in\overline{\alg(Y,C_{\ell},C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

we call
\nu
the
right bi-free conjugate variable for
X
with respect to
\eta
and
\tau
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
and denote
\nu
by
J_{r}(Y:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
.

Example 5.2
.

For an example of Definition
5.1
, consider
X\in A_{\ell}
,
Y\in A_{r}
,
Z_{2},Z_{3}\in C_{\ell}
, and
Z_{1},Z_{4}\in C_{r}
. If
\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},\alg(C_{r},Y)),\eta)
, then

\displaystyle\tau(XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}XZ_{4}\xi)

\displaystyle=\tau\left(Z_{1}YYZ_{4}L_{\eta(E(Z_{2}XZ_{3}X))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tau\left(XZ_{1}Z_{2}YYZ_{4}L_{\eta(E(Z_{3}X))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tau\left(XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}Z_{4}L_{\eta(E(1))}\right)

This can be observed diagrammatically by drawing
X,Z_{1},Z_{2},Y,X,Y,Z_{3},X,Z_{4}
as one would in a bi-non-crossing diagram (i.e. drawing two vertical lines and placing the variables on these lines starting at the top and going down with left variables on the left line and right variables on the right line), drawing all pictures connecting the centre of the bottom of the diagram to any
X
, taking the product of the elements starting from the top and going down in each of the two isolated components of the diagram, taking the expectation of the bounded region and applying
\eta
to the result to obtain a
b\in B
, appending
L_{b}
to the end of the product of operators from the unbounded region, and applying
\tau
to the result.

Z_{1}
Y
Y
Z_{4}
X
Z_{2}
X
Z_{3}
X

Z_{1}
Y
Y
Z_{4}
X
Z_{2}
X
Z_{3}
X

Z_{1}
Y
Y
Z_{4}
X
Z_{2}
X
Z_{3}
X

This is analogous to applying the left bi-free difference quotient
\partial_{\ell,X}
defined in
[
CS2020
]
on a suitable algebraic free product to
XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}XZ_{4}
to obtain

Z_{1}YYZ_{4}\otimes Z_{2}XZ_{3}Z+XZ_{1}Z_{2}YYZ_{4}\otimes Z_{3}X+XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}Z_{4}\otimes 1,

applying
\mathrm{Id}\otimes(\eta\circ E)
, collapsing the tensor, and applying
\tau
to the result.

Similarly, if
\nu=J_{r}(Y:(\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r}),\eta)
, then

\displaystyle\tau(XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}XZ_{4}\nu)

\displaystyle=\tau\left(XZ_{1}Z_{2}XZ_{3}XR_{\eta(E(YZ_{4}))}\right)+\tau\left(XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXZ_{3}XR_{\eta(E(Z_{4}))}\right).

This can be observed diagrammatically in a similar fashion by drawing all pictures connecting the centre of the bottom of the diagram to any
Y
on the right.

Z_{1}
Y
Y
Z_{4}
X
Z_{2}
X
Z_{3}
X

Z_{1}
Y
Y
Z_{4}
X
Z_{2}
X
Z_{3}
X

This is analogous to applying the right bi-free difference quotient
\partial_{r,Y}
defined in
[
CS2020
]
on a suitable algebraic free product to
XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXYZ_{3}XZ_{4}
to obtain

XZ_{1}Z_{2}XZ_{3}X\otimes YZ_{4}+XZ_{1}Z_{2}YXZ_{3}X\otimes Z_{4},

applying
\mathrm{Id}\otimes(\eta\circ E)
, collapsing the tensor, and applying
\tau
to the result.

Remark 5.3
.

(i)

As
\tau(aL_{b})=\tau(aR_{b})
for all
a\in A
and
b\in B
, one may use either
L_{\eta\circ E}
or
R_{\eta\circ E}
in either part of Definition
5.1
. In fact, one may simply use
\eta\circ E
if one views the resulting element of
B
as an element of
L_{2}(B,\tau_{B})\subseteq L_{2}(A,\tau)
, since
\tau(aL_{b})=\tau(a(b+N_{\tau}))
for all
a\in A
and
b\in B
by construction.

(ii)

The element
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
is unique in the sense that if
\xi_{0}\in\overline{\alg(X,C_{\ell},C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variable relations for
X
with respect to
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
, then
\xi_{0}=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
as the left bi-free conjugate variable relations causes the inner products in
L_{2}(A,\tau)
of both
\xi_{0}
and
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
against any element of
\alg(X,C_{\ell},C_{r})
to be equal.

(iii)

In the case where
B=\mathbb{C}
,
E
reduces down to a unital, linear map
\varphi:A\to\mathbb{C}
and, as
\tau
is compatible with
E
, one obtains that
\tau=\varphi
. As
\varphi
is linear, Definition
5.1
immediately reduces down to the left and right conjugate variables with respect to
\varphi
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
as in
[
CS2020
]
, provided
\eta
is unital.

(iv)

In the setting of Example
2.6
, we note that
J_{\ell}(X:(B_{\ell},B_{r}),\eta)
exists if and only if the free conjugate variable of
X
with respect to
(B,\eta)
from
[
S2000
]
exists. This immediately follows as
B_{r}
commutes with
X
and
B_{\ell}
and
\tau
is tracial, so the expressions for either conjugate variable can be modified into the expressions of the other conjugate variable.

As with the bi-free conjugate variables in
[
CS2020
]
, any moment expression should be equivalent to certain cumulant expressions via Möbius inversion. Thus we obtain the following equivalent characterization of conjugate variables. Note in that which follows, it does not matter whether the last entry in the analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulant function is treated as a left or as a right operator by Definition
4.1
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, let
Y\in A_{r}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. For
\xi\in L_{2}(A,\tau)
, the following are equivalent:

(i)

\xi
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variables relations for
X
(respectively
\xi
satisfies the right bi-free conjugate variables relations for
Y
) with respect to
\eta
and
\tau
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
,

(ii)

the following four cumulant conditions hold:

(a)

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(\xi)=0+N_{\tau_{B}}
,

(b)

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},\xi)=\eta(b)+N_{\tau_{B}}
(respectively
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,r)}}(YR_{b},\xi)=\eta(b)+N_{\tau_{B}}
) for all
b\in B
,

(c)

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(c_{1},\xi)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(c_{2},\xi)=0+N_{\tau_{B}}
for all
c_{1}\in C_{\ell}
and
c_{2}\in C_{r}
,

(d)

for all
n\geq 3
,
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n}
, and all
Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{n-1}\in A
such that

Z_{k}\in\begin{cases}\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}&\text{if }\chi(k)=\ell\\
C_{r}&\text{if }\chi(k)=r\\
\end{cases}\qquad\left(\text{respectively}\quad Z_{k}\in\begin{cases}C_{\ell}&\text{if }\chi(k)=\ell\\
\{Y\}\cup C_{r}&\text{if }\chi(k)=r\\
\end{cases}\right)

we have that

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)=0+N_{\tau_{B}}.

Proof.

We will prove the result for the left bi-free conjugate variable as the proof for the right bi-free conjugate variable is analogous.

Suppose that
\xi
satisfies (ii). To see that
\xi
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variables relations, let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\cup C_{r}
. Fix
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n+1}
such that

\displaystyle\chi(k)=\begin{cases}\ell&\text{if }Z_{k}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\\
r&\text{if }Z_{k}\in C_{r}\end{cases}

(note the value of
\chi(n+1)
does not matter in that which follows). By the relation between the analytic extensions of the bi-moment and bi-cumulant functions, we obtain that

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{n}\xi)=\sum_{\pi\in\BNC(\chi)}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n},\xi).

Due to the cumulant conditions in (ii), the only way
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n},\xi)
is non-zero is if the block of
\pi
containing
n+1
contains a single other index
k
with
Z_{k}=X
. Moreover, there is a bijection between such partitions and partitions of the form

\pi=\{k,n+1\}\cup\pi_{1}\cup\pi_{2}

where
\pi_{1}
is a bi-non-crossing partition on
V_{k}=\{k<m<n+1\,\mid\,Z_{m}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\}
with respect to
\chi|_{V_{k}}
and where
\pi_{2}
is a bi-non-crossing partition on
W_{k}=V_{k}\setminus\{k,n+1\}
with respect to
\chi|_{W_{k}}
. Using this decomposition, the properties of bi-analytic extensions of bi-multiplicative functions and the moment-cumulant formulas yield that

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}(Z_{1}\cdots Z_{n}\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\sum_{\pi_{2}\in\BNC(\chi|_{W_{k}})}\sum_{\pi_{1}\in\BNC(\chi|_{V_{k}})}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\{k,n+1\}\cup\pi_{1}\cup\pi_{2}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n},\xi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\sum_{\pi_{2}\in\BNC(\chi|_{W_{k}})}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\{k,n+1\}\cup\pi_{1}}\left(\left.\left(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k-1},Z_{k}L_{E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)}Z_{k+1},\ldots,Z_{n},\xi\right)\right|_{W_{k}\cup\{k,n+1\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi_{1}}\left(\left.\left(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\max_{\leq}(W_{k})-1},Z_{\max_{\leq}(W_{k})}\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)\right)\right|_{W_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tilde{E}\left(\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n+1\}}Z_{p}\right)\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)\right).

Hence, by applying
\tau_{B}
to both sides of this equation, the left bi-free conjugate variables relations from Definition
5.1
are obtained via part (i) of Proposition
3.9
.

For the converse direction, suppose
\xi
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variables relations for
X
. Thus for all
b\in B
,
\tau(L_{b}\xi)=0
by the conjugate variable relations. Hence
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(\xi)=\widetilde{E}(\xi)=0
by part (vi) of Proposition
3.9
and therefore (a) holds.

To see that (b) holds, note for all
b_{0},b\in B
that

\displaystyle b_{0}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},\xi)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b},\xi)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b},\xi)-\Psi_{0_{(\ell,\ell)}}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b},\xi)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{E}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b}\xi)-E(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b})\widetilde{E}(\xi)=\widetilde{E}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b}\xi).

Therefore, by applying
\tau_{B}
to both sides, we obtain that

\tau_{B}\left(b_{0}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},\xi)\right)=\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{E}(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b}\xi)\right)=\tau(L_{b_{0}}XL_{b}\xi).

By the left bi-free conjugate variable relations we obtain that

\displaystyle\tau_{B}\left(b_{0}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},\xi)\right)=\tau\left(L_{b_{0}}L_{\eta(E(L_{b}))}\right)=\tau(L_{b_{0}}L_{\eta(b)})=\tau(L_{b_{0}\eta(b)})=\tau_{B}(b_{0}\eta(b)).

As this holds for all
b_{0}\in B
, we obtain that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},\xi)=\eta(b)+N_{\tau_{B}}
as desired.

To see that (c) holds, note for all
b\in B
and
c_{1}\in C_{\ell}
that

\tau_{B}\left(b\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(c_{1},\xi)\right)=\tau(L_{b}c_{1}\xi)=0

by similar computations as above. Since this holds for all
b\in B
, we see that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(c_{1},\xi)=0+N_{\tau_{B}}
. Similarly, for all
c_{2}\in C_{r}
we see that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(c_{2},\xi)b=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(R_{b}c_{2},\xi)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(R_{b}c_{2},\xi)-\Psi_{0_{(r,\ell)}}(R_{b}c_{2},\xi)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{E}(R_{b}c_{2}\xi)-\widetilde{E}(\xi)E(R_{b}c_{2})=\widetilde{E}(R_{b}c_{2}\xi).

Therefore, by applying
\tau_{B}
to both sides, we obtain that

\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(c_{2},\xi)b\right)=\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{E}(R_{b}c_{2}\xi)\right)=\tau(R_{b}c_{2}\xi).

By the left bi-free conjugate variable relations we obtain that

\displaystyle\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(c_{2},\xi)b\right)=0.

Therefore, as
\tau_{B}
is tracial and the above holds for all
b\in B
, we obtain that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(c_{2},\xi)=0+N_{\tau_{B}}
as desired.

For (d), we proceed by induction on
n
. To do so, we will prove the base case
n=3
and the inductive step simultaneously. Fix
n\geq 3
and suppose when
n>3
that (d) holds for all
m<n
. Let
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{n}
and let
Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{n-1}\in A
be as in the assumptions of (d). We will assume that
\chi(1)=r
as the case
\chi(1)=\ell
will be handled similarly. Thus for all
b\in B
we know that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)b

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(R_{b}Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{E}(R_{b}Z_{1}Z_{2}\cdots Z_{n-1}\xi)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\pi\neq 1_{\chi}\\
\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(R_{b}Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{E}(R_{b}Z_{1}Z_{2}\cdots Z_{n-1}\xi)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\pi\neq 1_{\chi}\\
\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)b.

Using the fact that (a), (b), (c) hold and that (d) holds for all
m<n
, we obtain using the same arguments used in the other direction of the proof that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\BNC(\chi)\\
\pi\neq 1_{\chi}\\
\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)b=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k<n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tilde{E}\left(R_{b}\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n\}}Z_{p}\right)\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)\right),

where
V_{k}=\{k<m<n\,\mid\,Z_{m}\in\{X\}\cup C_{\ell}\}
. Therefore, by applying
\tau_{B}
to both sides of our initial equation, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n-1},\xi)b\right)

\displaystyle=\tau_{B}\left(\widetilde{E}(R_{b}Z_{1}Z_{2}\cdots Z_{n-1}\xi)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k<n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tilde{E}\left(R_{b}\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n\}}Z_{p}\right)\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\tau(R_{b}Z_{1}Z_{2}\cdots Z_{n-1}\xi)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k<n\\
Z_{k}=X\end{subarray}}\tau\left(R_{b}\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}^{c}\setminus\{k,n\}}Z_{p}\right)\eta\left(E\left(\prod_{p\in V_{k}}Z_{p}\right)\right)\right)=0,

by the left bi-free conjugate variable relations. Therefore, as the above holds for all
b\in B
and
\tau_{B}
is tracial, the result follows.
∎

The cumulant approach to conjugate variables has merits as it is very simple to check that most cumulants vanish and the values of others. For instance, an observant reader might have noticed that the operators
X
and
Y
in Definition
5.1
were not required to be self-adjoint. This is for later use in the paper and can be converted to studying self-adjoint operators as follows.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. The left bi-free conjugate variables

J_{\ell}(X:(\alg(C_{\ell},X^{*}),C_{r}),\eta)\qquad\text{and}\qquad J_{\ell}(X^{*}:(\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r}),\eta)

exist if and only if

J_{\ell}(\Re(X):(\alg(C_{\ell},\Im(X)),C_{r}),\eta)\qquad\text{and}\qquad J_{\ell}(\Im(X):(\alg(C_{\ell},\Re(X)),C_{r}),\eta)

exist where
\Re(X)=\frac{1}{2}(X+X^{*})
and
\Im(X)=\frac{1}{2i}(X-X^{*})
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle J_{\ell}(\Re(X):(\alg(C_{\ell},\Im(X)),C_{r}),\eta)

\displaystyle=J_{\ell}(X:(\alg(C_{\ell},X^{*}),C_{r}),\eta)+J_{\ell}(X^{*}:(\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r}),\eta)

\displaystyle J_{\ell}(\Im(X):(\alg(C_{\ell},\Re(X)),C_{r}),\eta)

\displaystyle=iJ_{\ell}(X:(\alg(C_{\ell},X^{*}),C_{r}),\eta)-iJ_{\ell}(X^{*}:(\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r}),\eta).

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

Suppose

\xi_{1}=J_{\ell}(X:(\alg(C_{\ell},X^{*}),C_{r}),\eta)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{2}=J_{\ell}(X^{*}:(\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r}),\eta)

exist. Hence
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
satisfy the appropriate analytic cumulant equations from Theorem
5.4
. Let

h_{1}=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad h_{2}=i\xi_{1}-i\xi_{2}.

As

\xi_{1}\in\overline{\alg(X,\alg(C_{\ell},X^{*}),C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{2}\in\overline{\alg(X^{*},\alg(C_{\ell},X),C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

we easily see that

h_{1}\in\overline{\alg(\Re(X),\alg(C_{\ell},\Im(X)),C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{2}\in\overline{\alg(\Im(X),\alg(C_{\ell},\Re(X)),C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}.

Thus, by Theorem
5.4
, it suffices to show that
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
satisfy the appropriate conjugate variable formulae. Indeed, property (a) of Theorem
5.4
holds as

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(h_{1})=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(h_{2})=0+N_{\tau_{B}}.

Next, notice for all
b\in B
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(\Re(X)L_{b},h_{1})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}XL_{b},\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}X^{*}L_{b},\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}XL_{b},\xi_{2}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}X^{*}L_{b},\xi_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\eta(b)+0+0+\frac{1}{2}\eta(b)=\eta(b),

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(\Im(X)L_{b},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2i}XL_{b},i\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(-\frac{1}{2i}X^{*}L_{b},i\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2i}XL_{b},-i\xi_{2}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(-\frac{1}{2i}X^{*}L_{b},-i\xi_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i}i\eta(b)-0+0-\frac{1}{2i}(-i)\eta(b)=\eta(b).

Hence property (b) of Theorem
5.4
holds.

To see properties (c) and (d) of Theorem
5.4
hold, note for all
b\in B
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(\Re(X)L_{b},h_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}XL_{b},i\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}X^{*}L_{b},i\xi_{1}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}XL_{b},-i\xi_{2}\right)+\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}\left(\frac{1}{2}X^{*}L_{b},-i\xi_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}i\eta(b)+0+0+\frac{1}{2}(-i)\eta(b)=0,

and similarly
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(\Im(X)L_{b},h_{1})=0
. Therefore, Proposition
4.16
along with the linearity of the cumulants in each entry yield properties (c) and (d).

The converse direction is proved analogously.
∎

Of course, many other results follow immediately from the cumulant definition of the conjugate variables.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. If

\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)

exists, then for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
the conjugate variable
J_{\ell}(\lambda X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
exists and is equal to
\frac{1}{\lambda}\xi
.

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}:B\to B
be completely positive maps. If

\xi_{1}=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{2}=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{2})

exist, then
\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{1}+\eta_{2})
exists and
\xi=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}
.

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. For fixed
b_{1},b_{2}\in B
, define
\eta_{\ell,b_{1},b_{2}}:B\to B
by

\eta_{\ell,b_{1},b_{2}}(b)=\eta(bb_{2})b_{1}

for all
b\in B
. If
\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
exists and
\eta_{\ell,b_{1},b_{2}}
is completely positive, then
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{\ell,b_{1},b_{2}})
exists and

J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{\ell,b_{1},b_{2}})=R_{b_{1}}L_{b_{2}}\xi.

Similarly, if
Y\in A_{r}
and
\eta_{r,b_{1},b_{2}}:B\to B
is defined by

\eta_{r,b_{1},b_{2}}=b_{2}\eta(b_{1}b)

for all
b\in B
is completely positive, and
J_{r}(Y:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
exists, then
J_{r}(Y:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{r,b_{1},b_{2}})
exists and

J_{r}(Y:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta_{r,b_{1},b_{2}})=R_{b_{1}}L_{b_{2}}J_{r}(Y:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta).

Proof.

By Theorem
5.4
, it suffices to show that
R_{b_{1}}L_{b_{2}}\xi
satisfies the appropriate analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulant formula. Indeed, clearly

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(R_{b_{1}}L_{b_{2}}\xi)=b_{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell)}}(\xi)b_{1}=0

and for all
b\in B

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b},R_{b_{1}}L_{b_{2}}\xi)=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{b}L_{b_{2}},\xi)b_{1}=\widetilde{\kappa}_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(XL_{bb_{2}},\xi)b_{1}=\eta(bb_{2})b_{1}=\eta_{b_{1},b_{2}}(b).

To show that the other analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulants from Theorem
5.4
vanish, one simply needs to use the analytical extension properties of bi-multiplicative functions together with Proposition
4.16
. The result for right bi-free conjugate variables is analogous.
∎

Similarly, many results pertaining to conjugate variables from
[
V1998-2
,
S2000
,
CS2020
]
immediately generalize to the conjugate variables in Definition
5.1
. However, one result from
[
S2000
]
requires additional set-up. In the context of Example
2.6
, one can always consider a further von Neumann subalgebra
D
of
B
and ask how the conjugate variables react. To analyze the comparable situation in our setting, we need the following example.

Example 5.9
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space and let
D
be a unital
*
-subalgebra of
B
(with
1_{D}=1_{B}
). If
F:B\to D
is a conditional expectation in the sense that
F(d)=d
for all
d\in D
and
F(d_{1}bd_{2})=d_{1}F(b)d_{2}
for all
d_{1},d_{2}\in D
and
b\in B
, then
(A,F\circ E,\varepsilon|_{D\otimes D^{{\text{op}}}})
is a
D
-
D
-non-commutative probability space by
[
S2016-2
]
*Section 3.

Note
\tau_{D}=\tau_{B}|_{D}:D\to\mathbb{C}
is a tracial state being the restriction of a tracial state. Moreover, if
\tau_{B}
is compatible with
F
in the sense that
\tau_{B}(F(b))=\tau_{B}(b)
for all
b\in B
, we easily see that
\tau
is compatible with
F\circ E
, as for all
a\in A
we have that

\tau(a)=\tau\left(L_{E(a)}\right)=\tau_{B}(E(a))=\tau_{B}(F(E(a)))=\tau\left(L_{F(E(a))}\right)

and similarly
\tau(a)=\tau\left(R_{F(E(a))}\right)
. Hence
(A,F\circ E,\varepsilon|_{D\otimes D^{{\text{op}}}},\tau)
is an analytical
D
-
D
-non-commutative probability space.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space and
X\in A_{\ell}
. In addition, let
D
and
F
be as in Example
5.9
and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras (and thus automatically a pair of
D
-algebras) and
\eta:D\to D
be a completely positive map. Moreover, suppose that
F
is completely positive (and hence
\eta\circ F:B\to D\subseteq B
is also completely positive).

Then, the conjugate variable
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta\circ F)
exists in the analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
if and only if the conjugate variable
J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
exists in the analytical
D
-
D
-non-commutative probability space
(A,F\circ E,\varepsilon|_{D\otimes D^{{\text{op}}}},\tau)
, in which case they are the same element of
L_{2}(A,\tau)
.

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

As
(\eta\circ F)\circ E=\eta\circ(F\circ E)
, the bi-free conjugate variable relations from Definition
5.1
are precisely the same and thus there is nothing to prove.
∎

With Proposition
5.10
out of the way, we turn our attention to proving that the expected generalizations of conjugate variable properties from
[
V1998-2
,
S2000
,
CS2020
]
hold.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. Suppose further that
D_{\ell}\subseteq C_{\ell}
and
D_{r}\subseteq C_{r}
are such that
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
is a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
. If

\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)

exists then

\xi^{\prime}=J_{\ell}(X:(D_{\ell},D_{r}),\eta)

exists. In particular, if
P
is the orthogonal projection of
L_{2}(A,\tau)
onto

\overline{\alg(X,D_{\ell},D_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

then
\xi^{\prime}=P(\xi)
.

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

Notice if
\xi
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variable relations for
X
with respect to
E
and
\eta
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
,
\xi
satisfies the left bi-free conjugate variable relations for
X
with respect to
E
and
\eta
in the presence of
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
. Therefore, since
\tau(ZP(\xi))=\tau(Z\xi)
for all
Z\in\alg(X,D_{\ell},D_{r})
, it follows that
P(\xi)=J_{\ell}(X:(D_{\ell},D_{r}),\eta)
as desired.
∎

The following generalizes
[
V1998-2
]
*Proposition 3.6,
[
S2000
]
*Proposition 3.8, and
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 4.3.

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras in
A
, let
X\in A_{\ell}
, and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. If
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
is another pair of
B
-algebras such that

(\alg(X,C_{\ell}),C_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(D_{\ell},D_{r})

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
, then

\xi=J_{\ell}(X:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)

exists if and only if

\xi^{\prime}=J_{\ell}(X:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),\eta)

exists, in which case they are equal.

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

Note by Lemma
5.11
that if
\xi^{\prime}
exists then
\xi
exists.

Conversely suppose that
\xi
exists. Hence
\xi
is an
\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}
-limit of elements from
\alg(X,C_{\ell},C_{r})
. Since the analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulants are
\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}
-continuous in the last entry, it follows that any analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulant involving
\xi
at the end and at least one element of
D_{\ell}
or
D_{r}
must be zero by Theorem
4.17
as

(\alg(X,C_{\ell}),C_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(D_{\ell},D_{r})

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
. Therefore, as

\xi\in\overline{\alg(X,C_{\ell},C_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}\subseteq\overline{\alg(X,\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r}))}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

it follows that
\xi^{\prime}
exists and
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
.
∎

The following generalizes
[
V1998-2
]
*Proposition 3.7,
[
S2000
]
*Proposition 3.11, and
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 4.4. In that which follows, we will use
\mathbf{Z}
to denote a tuple of operators
(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k})
. Furthermore, given another tuple
\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}=(Z^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Z^{\prime}_{k})
, we will use
\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}^{\prime}
to denote the tuple
(Z_{1}+Z^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}+Z^{\prime}_{k})
and we will use
\widehat{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
to denote the tuple
(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1},Z_{p+1},\ldots,Z_{k})
obtained by removing
Z_{p}
from the list.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space and let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map. Suppose
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
are
n
-tuples of operators from
A_{\ell}
,
\mathbf{Y}
and
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}
are
m
-tuples of operators from
A_{r}
, and
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
and
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
are pairs of
B
-algebras such that

(\alg(\mathbf{X},C_{\ell}),\alg(\mathbf{Y},C_{r}))\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\alg(\mathbf{X}^{\prime},D_{\ell}),\alg(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},D_{r}))

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
. If

\xi=J_{\ell}\left(X_{1}:\left(\alg\left(\widehat{\mathbf{X}_{1}},C_{\ell}\right),\alg(\mathbf{Y},C_{r})\right),\eta,\tau\right)

exists then

\xi^{\prime}=J_{\ell}\left(X_{1}+X^{\prime}_{1}:\left(\alg\left(\widehat{(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime})_{1}},C_{\ell},D_{\ell}\right),\alg(\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},C_{r},D_{r})\right)\right)

exists. Moreover, if
P
is the orthogonal projection of
L_{2}(\mathcal{A},\varphi)
onto

\overline{\alg(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},C_{\ell},C_{r},D_{\ell},D_{r})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

then

\xi^{\prime}=P(\xi).

A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

Suppose
\xi
exists. For notation purposes, let
\mathcal{A}=\alg(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},C_{\ell},C_{r},D_{\ell},D_{r})
.

Since
\tau(L_{b}ZP(\xi))=\tau(L_{b}Z\xi)
for all
Z\in\mathcal{A}
and
b\in B
(as
B_{\ell}\subseteq C_{\ell}
), we obtain by Proposition
3.9
that
\widetilde{E}(ZP(\xi))=\widetilde{E}(Z\xi)
for all
Z\in\mathcal{A}
. Thus, as
B_{\ell},B_{r}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
, we obtain for all
\chi\in\{\ell,r\}^{p}
with
\chi(p)=\ell
, for all
\pi\in\BNC(\chi)
, and for all
Z_{k}\in\mathcal{A}
with

Z_{k}\in\begin{cases}\alg(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime},C_{\ell},D_{\ell})&\text{if }\chi(k)=\ell\\
\alg(\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},C_{r},D_{r})&\text{if }\chi(k)=r\end{cases}

that
\Psi_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1}P(\xi))=\Psi_{\pi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1},\xi)
and thus

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1},P(\xi))=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1},\xi).

To show that
P(\xi)
is the appropriate left bi-free conjugate variable, it suffices to consider expressions of the form
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\chi}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{p-1},P(\xi))
and show they obtain the correct values as dictated in Theorem
5.4
. By the above, said cumulant is equal to an analytic operator-valued bi-free cumulant involving elements from
C_{\ell}
,
D_{\ell}
,
C_{r}
,
D_{r}
,
\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
, and
\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}
(in the appropriate positions) and a
\xi
at the end. By expanding using linearity, said cumulant can be modified to a sum of cumulants involving only elements from
C_{\ell}
,
D_{\ell}
,
C_{r}
,
D_{r}
,
\mathbf{X}
,
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{Y}
, and
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}
with a
\xi
at the end. By a similar argument to that in Lemma
5.5
, these cumulants then obtain the necessary values for
P(\xi)
to be the appropriate left bi-free conjugate variable due to Theorem
5.4
applied to
\xi
and the fact that

(\alg(\mathbf{X},C_{\ell}),\alg(\mathbf{Y},C_{r}))\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\alg(\mathbf{X}^{\prime},D_{\ell}),\alg(\mathbf{Y}^{\prime},D_{r}))

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
, so mixed cumulants vanish by Theorem
4.17
.
∎

6.
Bi-Semicircular Operators with Completely Positive Covariance

One essential example of conjugate variables in
[
V1998-2
,
S1998
,
CS2020
]
comes from central limit distributions. Thus this section is devoted to defining the operator-valued bi-semicircular operators with covariance coming from a completely positive map, showing that one may add in certain bi-semicircular operators into analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability spaces, and showing the bi-free conjugate variables behave in the appropriate manner.

To begin, let
B
be a unital
∗
-algebra and let
K
be a finite index set. For each
k\in K
let
Z_{k}
be a symbol. Recall the
full Fock space

\mathcal{F}(B,K)
is the algebraic free product of
B
and
\{Z_{k}\}_{k\in K}
; that is

\mathcal{F}(B,K)=B\oplus H_{1}\oplus H_{2}\oplus\cdots

where

H_{m}=\{b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m}\,\mid\,b_{0},b_{1},\ldots,b_{m}\in B,k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in K\}.

Note
\mathcal{F}(B,K)
is a
B
-
B
-bimodule with the obvious left and right actions of
B
on
B
and
H_{m}
. Moreover, as
\mathcal{F}(B,K)
is a direct sum of
B
and another
B
-
B
-bimodule,
\mathcal{F}(B,K)
is a
B
-
B
-bimodule with the specified vector state
p:\mathcal{F}(B,K)\to B
(as in the sense of
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Definition 3.1.1) defined by taking the
B
-term in the above direct product. Therefore, the set
A
of linear maps on
\mathcal{F}(B,K)
is a
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space with respect to the expectation
E:A\to B
defined by
E(T)=p(T1_{B})
(see
[
CNS2015-1
]
*Remark 3.2.2).

Let
\{\eta_{i,j}\}_{i,j\in K}
be linear maps on
B
. For each
k\in K
, the
left creation and annihilation operators

l_{k}
and
l^{*}_{k}
are the linear maps defined such that

\displaystyle l_{k}b

\displaystyle=1_{B}Z_{k}b

\displaystyle l_{k}(b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m})

\displaystyle=1_{B}Z_{k}b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m}

\displaystyle l^{*}_{k}b

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle l^{*}_{k}(b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m})

\displaystyle=\eta_{k,k_{1}}(b_{0})b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m}

and the
right creation and annihilation operators

r_{k}
and
r_{k}^{*}
are the linear maps defined such that

\displaystyle r_{k}b

\displaystyle=bZ_{k}1_{B}

\displaystyle r_{k}(b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m})

\displaystyle=b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m}Z_{k}1_{B}

\displaystyle r^{*}_{k}b

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle r^{*}_{k}(b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots Z_{k_{m}}b_{m})

\displaystyle=b_{0}Z_{k_{1}}b_{1}\cdots b_{m-1}\eta_{k_{m},k}(b_{m})

It is elementary to see that
l_{k},l_{k}^{*}\in A_{\ell}
and
r_{k},r_{k}^{*}\in A_{r}
. With these operators in hand, we make the following definition.

Definition 6.1
.

Using the above notation, write
K
as the disjoint union of two sets
I
and
J
. For each
i\in I
and
j\in J
, let

S_{i}=l_{i}+l^{*}_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad D_{j}=r_{j}+r^{*}_{j}.

The pair
(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
are called the
operator-valued bi-semicircular operators with covariance
\{\eta_{i,j}\}_{i,j\in K}
.

In the case that
\eta_{k_{1},k_{2}}=0
for all
k_{1},k_{2}\in K
with
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
, we say that
(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
is a collection of
(\{\eta_{i,i}\}_{i\in I},\{\eta_{j,j}\}_{j\in J})
bi-semicircular operators
.

Remark 6.2
.

It is natural to ask what are the necessary conditions for operator-valued bi-semicircular operators with covariance
\{\eta_{i,j}\}_{i,j\in K}
to sit inside an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space. One may hope that a condition similar to
[
S1998
]
*Theorem 4.3.1 would work; that is, the answer is yes if
\tau_{B}
is tracial and
\eta:M_{|K|}(B)\to M_{|K|}(B)
defined by

\eta([b_{i,j}]_{i,j\in K})=[\eta_{i,j}(b_{i,j})]_{i,j\in K}

is completely positive. However, if
i\in I
,
j\in J
, and
b_{1},b_{2}\in B
, it is not difficult to verify that

\tau_{B}\left(E((S_{i}D_{j}L_{b_{1}}R_{b_{2}})^{*}(S_{i}D_{j}L_{b_{1}}R_{b_{2}})\right)=\tau_{B}\left(b_{2}^{*}\eta_{i,i}(1_{B})b_{1}b_{2}\eta_{j,j}(1_{B})b_{1}^{*}+b_{2}^{*}\eta_{i,j}(\eta_{i,j}(b_{1}b_{2}))b_{1}^{*}\right)

and it is not clear if this is positive (even if the outer
\eta_{i,j}
was a
\eta_{j,i}
).

Only certain operator-valued bi-semicircular operators are required in this paper. Indeed, we will need only the case where
(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
are
(\{\eta_{i,i}\}_{i\in I},\{\eta_{j,j}\}_{j\in J})
bi-semicircular operators, as in this setting the pairs of algebras

\{(\alg(B_{\ell},S_{i}),B_{r})\}_{i\in I}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},D_{j}))\}_{j\in J}

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
, as the following result shows.

Theorem 6.3
.

Using the above notation, if
B
is a
*
-algebra,
\tau_{B}
is a tracial state on
B
,
\{\eta_{i}\}_{i\in I}\cup\{\eta_{j}\}_{j\in J}
are completely positive maps from
B
to
B
,
(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
are
(\{\eta_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\eta_{j}\}_{j\in J})
bi-semicircular operators,
A_{\ell}=\alg(B_{\ell},\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I})
,
A_{r}=\alg(B_{r},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
,
A
is generated as a
*
-algebra by
A_{\ell}
and
A_{r}
and
\tau:A\to\mathbb{C}
is defined by
\tau=\tau_{B}\circ E
, then
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
is an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space. Moreover, all operator-valued bi-free cumulants involving
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of order not two are zero and for all
i,i_{1},i_{2}\in I
and
j,j_{1},j_{2}\in J
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{1_{(\ell,\ell)}}(S_{i_{1}}L_{b},S_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{i_{1},i_{2}}\eta_{i_{1}}(b)

\displaystyle\kappa_{1_{(r,r)}}(D_{j_{1}}R_{b},D_{j_{2}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{j_{1},j_{2}}\eta_{j_{1}}(b)

\displaystyle\kappa_{1_{(\ell,r)}}(S_{i}L_{b},D_{j})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\kappa_{1_{(r,\ell)}}(D_{j}R_{b},S_{i})

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

As shown above,
(A,E,\varepsilon)
is a
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space. Next, note that
E
is completely positive when restricted
A_{\ell}
and
A_{r}
by
[
S1998
]
*Remark 4.3.2 as the expectations reduce to the free case. In fact, this same idea can be used to show that
\tau
is positive. Indeed, first note that
S_{i}
and
D_{j}
commute. Thus every element of
A
can be written as sum of elements of the form

Z=L_{b_{0}}S_{i_{1}}L_{b_{1}}\cdots S_{i_{n}}L_{b_{n}}R_{b_{n+1}}D_{i_{n+1}}R_{b_{n+2}}\cdots D_{j_{n+m}}R_{b_{n+m}}

where
b_{0},\ldots b_{n+m}\in B
. Moreover, we can write

\mathcal{F}(B,K)\cong\mathcal{F}(B,I)\otimes_{B}\mathcal{F}(B,J)

in such a way that
Z
acts via

L_{b_{0}}S_{i_{1}}L_{b_{1}}\cdots S_{i_{n}}L_{b_{n}}\otimes L_{b_{n+m}}S_{j_{n+m}}L_{b_{n+m-1}}\cdots S_{j_{n+1}}L_{b_{n+1}},

so that if
E_{I}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{F}(B,I))\to B
and
E_{J}:\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{F}(B,J))\to B
are the corresponding expectations, then

E(Z)=E_{I}(L_{b_{0}}S_{i_{1}}L_{b_{1}}\cdots S_{i_{n}}L_{b_{n}})E_{J}(L_{b_{n+m}}S_{j_{n+m}}L_{b_{n+m-1}}\cdots S_{j_{n+1}}L_{b_{n+1}}).

Therefore, if we have
Z=\sum^{d}_{k=1}X_{k}Y_{k}
where
X_{k}
is a product of
L_{b}
’s and
S_{i}
’s and
Y_{k}
is a product of
R_{b}
’s and
D_{j}
’s, then

\tau(Z^{*}Z)=\sum^{d}_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}\tau_{B}\left(E_{I}\left(X_{k_{1}}^{*}X_{k_{2}}\right)E_{J}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{Y}^{*}_{k_{1}}\right)\right),

where
\widetilde{Y}
represents the monomial obtained by reversing the order and changing
R
’s to
L
’s and
D
’s to
S
’s. However, as
E_{I}
and
E_{J}
are completely positive on the algebras generated by
L
’s and
S
’s, we can find
b_{X,k_{1},k_{3}},b_{Y,k_{1},k_{4}}\in B
such that

E_{I}\left(X_{k_{1}}^{*}X_{k_{2}}\right)=\sum^{d_{2}}_{k_{3}=1}b^{*}_{X,k_{1},k_{3}}b_{X,k_{2},k_{3}}\quad\text{and}\quad E_{J}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{k_{2}}\widetilde{Y}^{*}_{k_{1}}\right)=\sum^{d_{3}}_{k_{4}=1}b_{Y,k_{2},k_{4}}b^{*}_{Y,k_{1},k_{4}},

thus

\displaystyle\tau(Z^{*}Z)

\displaystyle=\sum^{d}_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}\sum^{d_{2}}_{k_{3}=1}\sum^{d_{3}}_{k_{4}=1}\tau_{B}(b^{*}_{X,k_{1},k_{3}}b_{X,k_{2},k_{3}}b_{Y,k_{2},k_{4}}b^{*}_{Y,k_{1},k_{4}})

\displaystyle=\sum^{d_{2}}_{k_{3}=1}\sum^{d_{3}}_{k_{4}=1}\tau_{B}\left(\left(\sum^{d}_{k_{1}=1}b_{X,k_{1},k_{3}}b_{Y,k_{1},k_{4}}\right)^{*}\left(\sum^{d}_{k_{2}=1}b_{X,k_{2},k_{3}}b_{Y,k_{2},k_{4}}\right)\right)\geq 0.

Hence
\tau
is positive.

Next, one can verify in
L_{2}(A,\tau)
that
S_{i}
and
D_{j}
are the sum of an isometry and its adjoint (see
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 4.6.9) and thus define bounded linear operators. Hence
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
is an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space.

To see the cumulant condition, one can proceed in two ways. One can immediately realize that

(\alg(B_{\ell},\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I}),B_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})

are bi-free over
B
due to the above tensor-product relation. This implies mixed cumulants are zero. The other cumulants then follow from the free case in
[
S1998
]
. Alternatively, one can analyze the actions of
L_{b}
,
R_{b}
,
S_{i}
, and
D_{j}
as one would on the operator-valued reduced free product space in an identical way to the LR-diagrams of
[
CNS2015-2
,
CNS2015-1
]
to obtain a diagrammatic description of the elements of
\mathcal{F}(B,K)
produced, note that the ones that contribute to a
B
-element are exactly the bi-non-crossing diagrams that correspond to pair bi-non-crossing partitions, and use induction to deduce the values of the operator-valued cumulants.
∎

We immediately obtain the following using Theorem
6.3
and Theorem
5.4
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, and let
(\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
be
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
bi-semicircular operators in
A
. Then

J_{\ell}\left(S_{1}:\left(\alg\left(B_{\ell},\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{j=2}\right),\alg\left(B_{r},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,1}\right)=S_{1}.

A similar result holds for the other left and the right conjugate variables.

In order to obtain more examples of bi-free conjugate variables, it would be typical to perturb by bi-semicircular operators and use Proposition
5.13
. To do this we must have the collection of bi-semicircular operators in the same analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space. Thus, it is natural to ask whether given two analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability spaces there is a bi-free product which causes the pairs of left and right algebras to be bi-freely independent over
B
and preserve the analytical properties.

Unfortunately we do not have an answer to this question. The proof of positivity in the operator-valued free case requires the characterization of the vanishing of alternating centred moments in
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 3.3.3 to ensure positivity in the end. One may attempt to use the bi-free analogue of ‘alternating centred moments vanish’ from
[
C2019
]
, however the bi-free formulae generalization of
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 3.3.3 is far more complicated. In particular, the proof from
[
S1998
]
will not immediately generalize, as Example
3.4
shows
E
will not be positive and the traciality of
\tau_{B}
will need to come into play.

Luckily, if we deal only with bi-semicircular operators, which is all that is required for this paper, there is no issue. In fact, in the case one is working with von Neumann factors as in Example
2.6
, the following is trivial as it we can add the corresponding collection of bi-semicircular operators using factors by
[
S1998
]
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space with
A_{\ell}
and
A_{r}
generated by isometries, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, and let
(\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
be
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
bi-semicircular operators. Then, there exists an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space
(A^{\prime},E^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
with
A\subseteq A^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}|_{A}=E
,
\tau^{\prime}|_{A}=\tau
,
A_{\ell}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{\ell}
,
A_{r}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{r}
,
\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{\ell}
,
\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{r}
and such that the pairs of algebras

(A_{\ell},A_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left(\alg(B_{\ell},\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}),\alg(B_{r},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1})\right)

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E^{\prime}
.

As for any
(\{\eta_{i,i}\}_{i\in I},\{\eta_{j,j}\}_{j\in J})
bi-semicircular operators
(\{S_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{D_{j}\}_{j\in J})
we know that

\{(\alg(B_{\ell},S_{i}),B_{r})\}_{i\in I}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},D_{j}))\}_{j\in J}

are bi-free over
B
, to prove Theorem
6.5
it suffices to use the following lemma and an analogous result on the right iteratively, or simply adapt the proof to multiple operators simultaneously.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space with
A_{\ell}
and
A_{r}
generated by isometries, let
\eta:B\to B
be a completely positive map and let
S
be an
\eta
-semicircular operator. Then, there exists an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space
(A^{\prime},E^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
such that
A^{\prime}=\alg(A_{\ell},A_{r},S)
,
E^{\prime}|_{A}=E
,
\tau^{\prime}|_{A}=\tau
,
A^{\prime}_{\ell}=\alg(A_{\ell},S)
,
A^{\prime}_{r}=A_{r}
and

(A_{\ell},A_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\alg(B_{\ell},S),B_{r})

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By taking the operator-valued bi-free product of
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability spaces, we obtain a
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space
(A^{\prime},E^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime})
such that
A^{\prime}=\alg(A_{\ell},A_{r},S)
,
A^{\prime}_{\ell}=\alg(A_{\ell},S)
,
A^{\prime}_{r}=A_{r}
,
E^{\prime}|_{A}=E
and

(A_{\ell},A_{r})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\alg(B_{\ell},S),B_{r})

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E^{\prime}
. Note
E^{\prime}
restricted to
A_{r}
is trivially completely positive and
E^{\prime}
restricted to
A^{\prime}_{\ell}
is completely positive by the free result from
[
S1998
]
. Thus, to verify Definition
3.1
it suffices to verify that if
\tau^{\prime}=\tau_{B}\circ E
, then
\tau^{\prime}
is positive and elements of
A^{\prime}_{\ell}
and
A^{\prime}_{r}
define bounded operators on
L_{2}(A^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
.

By analyzing the reduced free product construction, we can realize
A_{\ell}
,
A_{r}
, and
S
as operators acting on

\mathcal{F}=B\oplus\mathcal{H}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{2}\oplus\cdots,

where

\mathcal{H}_{m}=\{a_{0}Za_{1}\cdots Za_{m}\,\mid\,a_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{m-1}\in A_{\ell},a_{m}\in A\}

and if
p:\mathcal{F}\to B
is defined by taking the
B
-term in
\mathcal{F}
, then

E^{\prime}(T)=p(T1_{B}).

Define a function
\langle\,\cdot,\cdot\,\rangle:\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{F}\to A
by setting
B
,
\mathcal{H}_{1}
,
\mathcal{H}_{2}
,
\ldots
. to be pairwise orthogonal,
\langle b_{1},b_{2}\rangle=L_{b_{2}^{*}b_{1}}
, and

\langle a^{\prime}_{0}Za^{\prime}_{1}\cdots Za^{\prime}_{m},a_{0}Za_{1}\cdots Za_{m}\rangle=a_{m}^{*}L_{\eta}(a_{m-1}^{*}\cdots L_{\eta}(a_{1}^{*}L_{\eta}(a_{0}^{*}a^{\prime}_{0})a^{\prime}_{1})\cdots a^{\prime}_{m-1})a^{\prime}_{m},

where
L_{\eta}(T)=L_{\eta(E(T))}
. As
\eta
is completely positive and
E
is completely positive when restricted to
A_{\ell}
, we obtain that
\langle\,\cdot,\cdot\,\rangle
is an
A
-valued inner product by the same arguments as
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 4.6.6. To elaborate slightly, given
\sum^{n}_{k=1}a_{k,0}Za_{k,1}\cdots Za_{k,m}
, the matrix
[\eta(a^{*}_{i,0}a_{j,0})]
is positive and thus can be written as
[\sum^{n}_{k=1}b^{*}_{k,i}b_{k,j}]
for some
b_{i,j}\in B
. One then substitutes
L_{\eta}(a^{*}_{i,0}a_{j,0})=\sum^{n}_{k=1}L_{b_{k,i}}^{*}L_{b_{k,j}}
and continues until one ends with a sum of products of elements of
A
with their adjoints.

As
\tau:A\to\mathbb{C}
is positive and as for all
T\in A^{\prime}
,

\tau^{\prime}(T^{*}T)=\tau(\langle T1_{B},T1_{B}\rangle),

we obtain that
\tau^{\prime}
is positive as desired. To see that elements of
A^{\prime}_{\ell}
and
A^{\prime}_{r}
define bounded operators on
L_{2}(A^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
note if
T\in A_{r}
, then, using the above description,

T(a_{0}Za_{1}\cdots Za_{m})=a_{0}Za_{1}\cdots ZTa_{m}.

As any of the terms

L_{\eta}(a_{m-1}^{*}\cdots L_{\eta}(a_{1}^{*}L_{\eta}(a_{0}^{*}a^{\prime}_{0})a^{\prime}_{1})\cdots a^{\prime}_{m-1})

in the above
A
-valued inner product will be able to be written as sums involving terms of the form
L_{b_{1}}^{*}L_{b_{2}}
and will then produce terms of the form

a_{m}^{*}T^{*}L_{b_{1}}^{*}L_{b_{2}}Ta^{\prime}_{m}=a_{m}^{*}L_{b_{1}}^{*}T^{*}TL_{b_{2}}a^{\prime}_{m}

in the
A
-valued inner product when
T
acts on the left, the fact that
A_{r}
is generated by isometries yields that
A_{r}
acts as bounded operators on
L_{2}(A,\tau)
. The fact that
A_{\ell}
is generated by isometries immediately yields that
A_{\ell}
acts as bounded operators on
L_{2}(A,\tau)
, and it is not difficult to see that
S
acts as the sum of an isometry and its adjoint on
L_{2}(A,\tau)
and thus is bounded.
∎

With Theorem
6.5
establishing we can always assume our
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability spaces have
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
bi-semicircular operators, we can proceed with the following.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}\in A_{r}^{m}
be tuples of self-adjoint operators, let
(\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
be
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
bi-semicircular operators in
A
and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be pairs of
B
-algebras of
A
such that

\{(\alg(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y})\rangle)\}\cup\{(\alg(B_{\ell},S_{i}),B_{r})\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},D_{j}))\}^{m}_{j=1}

are bi-free. If
P
is the orthogonal projection of
L_{2}(A,\varphi)
onto

\overline{\alg\left(C_{\ell},C_{r},\mathbf{X}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathbf{S},\mathbf{Y}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathbf{D}\right)}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}},

then

\xi=J_{\ell}\left(X_{1}+\sqrt{\epsilon}S_{1}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\widehat{(\mathbf{X}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathbf{S})}_{1}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\mathbf{Y}+\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathbf{D}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,1}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}P(S_{1}).

Thus

\left\|\xi\right\|_{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\sqrt{\tau_{B}(\eta(1_{B}))}.

A similar computation holds for the other entries of the tuples and the right conjugate variables.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.4
and Lemma
5.6
, we have that

\displaystyle J_{\ell}\left(\sqrt{\epsilon}S_{1}:\left(\alg\left(B_{\ell},\sqrt{\epsilon}\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{1}\right),\alg\left(B_{r},\sqrt{\epsilon}\mathbf{D}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,1}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}S_{1}.

The conjugate variable result then follows from Propositions
5.12
and
5.13
, whereas the
\tau
-norm computation is trivial.
∎

7.
Bi-Free Fisher Information with Respect to a Completely Positive Map

With the above technology, the bi-free Fisher information with respect to completely positive maps can be constructed and has similar properties to the bi-free Fisher information from
[
CS2020
]
and the free Fisher information with respect to a completely positive map from
[
S1998
]
. We highlight the main results and properties in this section.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}\in A_{r}^{m}
and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras of
A
. The
relative bi-free Fisher information of
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})
with respect to
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
in the presence of
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
is

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\left\|\xi_{i}\right\|_{\tau}^{2}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\left\|\nu_{j}\right\|_{\tau}^{2}

where for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}

\xi_{i}=J_{\ell}\left(X_{i}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\mathbf{Y}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,i}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\nu_{j}=J_{r}\left(Y_{j}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}_{j}\right)\right),\eta_{r,j}\right)

provided these variables exist, and otherwise is defined as
\infty
.

In the case that
\eta_{\ell,i}=\eta_{r,j}=\eta
for all
i
and
j
, we use
\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),\eta)
to denote the above bi-free Fisher information. In the case that
C_{\ell}=B_{\ell}
and
C_{r}=B_{r}
, we use
\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))
. In the case both occur, we use
\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:\eta)
.

Note the bi-free Fisher with respect to completely positive maps exists in many settings due to Theorem
6.5
and Theorem
6.7
. Furthermore, the properties of the bi-free Fisher with respect to completely positive maps are in analogy with those from
[
V1998-2
,
S1998
,
CS2020
]
as the following shows.

Remark 7.2
.

(i)

In the case that
B=\mathbb{C}
and
\eta
is unital, Definition
7.1
immediately reduces down to the bi-free Fisher information in
[
CS2020
]
*Definition 5.1 by Remark
5.3
.

(ii)

In the case we are in the context of Example
2.6
with
m=0
,
C_{\ell}=B_{\ell}
, and
C_{r}=B_{r}
, Definition
7.1
immediately reduces down to the free Fisher information with respect to a complete positive map from
[
S1998
]
*Definition 4.1.

(iii)

Note

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}(

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\Phi^{*}\left(X_{i}\sqcup\emptyset:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\mathbf{Y}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,i},\tau\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\Phi^{*}\left(\emptyset\sqcup Y_{j}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}_{j}\right)\right),\eta_{r,j},\tau\right).

(iv)

If
\mathbf{X}=(X_{1},X_{1}^{*},\ldots,X_{n},X_{n}^{*})
and
\mathbf{Y}=(Y_{1},Y_{1}^{*},\ldots,Y_{m},Y_{m}^{*})
, then Lemma
5.5
implies

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))=\frac{1}{2}\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))

where
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}=(\Re(X_{1}),\Im(X_{1}),\ldots,\Re(X_{n}),\Im(X_{n}))
and
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\Re(Y_{1}),\Im(Y_{1}),\ldots,\Re(Y_{m}),\Im(Y_{m}))
.

(v)

In the context of Proposition
5.10
(i.e. reducing the
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space to a
D
-
D
-non-commutative probability space),

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))=\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\circ F\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\circ F\}^{m}_{j=1})).

(vi)

By Lemma
5.6
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}

\Phi^{*}(\lambda\mathbf{X}\sqcup\lambda\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))=\frac{1}{|\lambda|^{2}}\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})).

(vii)

In the context of Lemma
5.11
(i.e.
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
a smaller pair of
B
-algebras than
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
),

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(D_{\ell},D_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))\leq\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})).

(viii)

In the context of Proposition
5.12
(i.e. adding in a bi-free pair of
B
-algebras),

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(\alg(D_{\ell},C_{\ell}),\alg(D_{r},C_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))=\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})).

(ix)

If in addition to the assumptions of Definition
7.1

\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\in A_{\ell}^{n^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\in A_{r}^{m^{\prime}}
,
(\{\eta^{\prime}_{\ell,i}\}^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1},\{\eta^{\prime}_{r,j}\}^{m^{\prime}}_{j=1})
is a collection of completely positive maps on
B
, and
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
is a pair of
B
-algebras then, by (iii) and (vii),

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}(

\displaystyle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{\eta^{\prime}_{\ell,i}\}^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}\cup\{\eta^{\prime}_{r,j}\}^{m^{\prime}}_{j=1}))

\displaystyle\geq\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))+\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(D_{\ell},D_{r}),(\{\eta^{\prime}_{\ell,i}\}^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1},\{\eta^{\prime}_{r,j}\}^{m^{\prime}}_{j=1}))

(x)

In the context of (ix) with the additional assumption that

\left(\alg(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y})\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left(\alg(D_{\ell},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}),\alg(D_{r},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime})\right)

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
, Proposition
5.12
implies that

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}(

\displaystyle\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{\eta^{\prime}_{\ell,i}\}^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}\cup\{\eta^{\prime}_{r,j}\}^{m^{\prime}}_{j=1}))

\displaystyle=\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))+\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(D_{\ell},D_{r}),(\{\eta^{\prime}_{\ell,i}\}^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1},\{\eta^{\prime}_{r,j}\}^{m^{\prime}}_{j=1}))

Unsurprisingly, more complicated properties of free Fisher information extend.

Proposition 7.3

(Bi-Free Stam Inequality)
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}\in A_{r}^{m}
, and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
and
(D_{\ell},D_{r})
be pairs of
B
-algebras of
A
such that

\left(\alg(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y})\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left(\alg(D_{\ell},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}),\alg(D_{r},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime})\right)

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}:(\alg(C_{\ell},D_{\ell}),\alg(C_{r},D_{r})),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))\right)^{-1}.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle P_{0}

\displaystyle:L_{2}(\mathcal{A},\varphi)\to L_{2}(B,\tau_{B})

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle:L_{2}(\mathcal{A},\varphi)\to\overline{\alg(C_{\ell},C_{r},\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle:L_{2}(\mathcal{A},\varphi)\to\overline{\alg(D_{\ell},D_{r},\mathbf{X}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}

be the orthogonal projections onto their co-domains. Note that if

Z\in\alg(C_{\ell},C_{r},\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})\quad\text{and}\quad\alg(D_{\ell},D_{r},\mathbf{X}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}),

then bi-freeness implies

\widetilde{E}(ZZ^{\prime})=\widetilde{E}\left(Z\widetilde{E}(Z^{\prime})\right).

Indeed, this is easily seen as if
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
are monomials, then any cumulant of the monomial
ZZ^{\prime}
corresponding to a bi-non-crossing partition is non-zero if and only if it decomposes into a bi-non-crossing partition on
Z
union a bi-non-crossing partition on
Z^{\prime}
. Thus
P_{1}P_{2}=P_{2}P_{1}=P_{0}
.

The remainder of the proof can then be read from
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 4.5,
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 5.8, or even
[
V1998-2
]
*Proposition 6.5.
∎

Proposition 7.4

(Bi-Free Cramer-Rao Inequality)
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}\in A_{r}^{m}
consist of self-adjoint operators and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras of
A
. Then

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))\tau\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}X_{i}^{2}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}Y_{j}^{2}\right)\geq\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{\ell,i}(1))+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{r,j}(1))\right)^{2}.

Moreover, equality holds if
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})
are
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
-bi-semicircular elements and

\{(C_{\ell},C_{r})\}\cup\{(\alg(B_{\ell},X_{i}),B_{r})\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},Y_{j}))\}^{m}_{j=1}

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
. The converse holds when
C_{\ell}=B_{\ell}
and
C_{r}=B_{r}
.

Proof.

The result follow from the obvious modifications to
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 5.10. Also see
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 4.6 and
[
V1998-2
]
*Proposition 6.9.
∎

Similarly, limits behave as one expects based on
[
CS2020
,
V1998-2
,
S1998
]
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}\in A_{r}^{m}
consist of self-adjoint operators and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be a pair of
B
-algebras of
A
. Suppose further for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
that
\mathbf{X}^{(k)}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}^{(k)}\in A_{r}^{m}
are tuples of self-adjoint elements in
A
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\to\infty}\left\|X^{(k)}_{i}\right\|<\infty,

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\to\infty}\left\|Y^{(k)}_{j}\right\|<\infty,

\displaystyle s\text{-}\lim_{k\to\infty}X^{(k)}_{i}=X_{i},\text{ and}

\displaystyle s\text{-}\lim_{k\to\infty}Y^{(k)}_{j}=Y_{j}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq m
(where the strong limit is computed as bounded linear maps acting on
L_{2}(A,\tau)
). Then

\displaystyle\liminf_{k\to\infty}\Phi^{*}\left(\mathbf{X}^{(k)}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}^{(k)}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})\right)\geq\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))

The proof of Proposition
7.5
becomes identical to
[
CS2020
]
*Proposition 5.12 once the following lemma is established. Also see
[
S1998
]
*Proposition 4.7 and
[
V1998-2
]
*Proposition 6.10.

Lemma 7.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
7.5
along with the additional assumptions that

\xi_{k}=J_{\ell}\left(X_{1}^{(k)}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\widehat{\mathbf{X}}^{(k)}_{1}\right),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y}^{(k)})\right),\eta_{\ell,1}\right)

exist and are bounded in
L_{2}
-norm by some constant
K>0
, it follows that

\xi=J_{\ell}\left(X_{1}:\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\widehat{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\right),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y})\right),\eta_{\ell,1}\right)

exists and is equal to

w\text{-}\lim_{k\to\infty}P\left(\xi_{k}\right)

where
P
is the orthogonal projection of
L_{2}(A,\tau)
onto
\overline{\alg(C_{\ell},C_{r},\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})}^{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\tau}}
.

If, in addition,

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\xi\right\|_{2}

then

\lim_{k\to\infty}\left\|\xi_{k}-\xi\right\|_{2}=0.

The same holds with
X_{1}
replaced with
X_{i}
, and a similar result holds for the right.

Proof.

The proof of this result follows from the same sequence of steps as
[
CS2020
]
*Lemma 5.13 using the analytical operator-valued bi-free cumulants.
∎

Corollary 7.7
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
7.5
, if in addition

(B_{\ell}(\mathbf{X}),B_{r}(\mathbf{Y}))\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left(\alg\left(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}^{(k)}\right),\alg\left(C_{r},\mathbf{Y}^{(k)}\right)\right)

are bi-free for all
k
and

\lim_{k\to\infty}\left\|X^{(k)}_{i}\right\|=\lim_{k\to\infty}\left\|Y_{j}^{(k)}\right\|=0

for all
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq m
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\Phi^{*}\left(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{(k)}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{(k)}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})\right)=\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})).

Furthermore, if
C_{\ell}=B_{\ell}
,
C_{r}=B_{r}
, and

\Phi^{*}(\mathbf{X}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}:(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}))<\infty,

then

J_{\ell}\left(X^{(k)}_{i}:\left(B_{\ell}\left(\widehat{(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{X}^{(k)})}_{i}\right),B_{r}\left(\mathbf{Y}+\mathbf{Y}^{(k)}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,i}\right)

tends to

J_{\ell}\left(X_{i}:\left(B_{\ell}\left(\hat{\mathbf{X}}_{i}\right),B_{r}\left(\mathbf{Y}\right)\right),\eta_{\ell,i}\right)

in
\tau
-norm. A similar result holds for right bi-free conjugate variables.

Proof.

The proof is identical to
[
CS2020
]
*Corollary 5.14 and thus is omitted.
∎

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
(A,E,\varepsilon,\tau)
be an analytical
B
-
B
-non-commutative probability space, let
\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1}
and
\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1}
be completely positive maps from
B
to
B
, let
\mathbf{X}\in A_{\ell}^{n}
and
\mathbf{Y}\in A_{r}^{m}
be tuples of self-adjoint operators, let
(\{S_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{D_{j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
be a collection of
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
bi-semicircular operators in
A
and let
(C_{\ell},C_{r})
be pairs of
B
-algebras of
A
such that

\{(\alg(C_{\ell},\mathbf{X}),\alg(C_{r},\mathbf{Y})\rangle)\}\cup\{(\alg(B_{\ell},S_{i}),B_{r})\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},D_{j}))\}^{m}_{j=1}

are bi-free. Then, the map

h:[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\Phi^{*}\left(\mathbf{X}+\sqrt{t}\mathbf{S}\sqcup\mathbf{Y}+\sqrt{t}\mathbf{D}:(C_{\ell},C_{r}),(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})\right)

is decreasing, right continuous, and

\frac{K_{2}^{2}}{K_{1}+K_{2}t}\leq h(t)\leq\frac{1}{t}K_{3}

where

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\tau\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}X_{i}^{2}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}Y_{j}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{\ell,i}(1_{B}))+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{r,j}(1_{B}))\text{ and}

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{\ell,i}(1_{B}))^{2}+\sum^{m}_{j=1}\tau_{B}(\eta_{r,j}(1_{B}))^{2}.

Moreover if
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})
is the
(\{\eta_{\ell,i}\}^{n}_{i=1},\{\eta_{r,j}\}^{m}_{j=1})
-bi-semicircular distribution and

\{(C_{\ell},C_{r})\}\cup\{(\alg(B_{\ell},X_{i}),B_{r})\}^{n}_{i=1}\cup\{(B_{\ell},\alg(B_{r},Y_{j}))\}^{m}_{j=1}

are bi-free with amalgamation over
B
with respect to
E
, then
h(t)=\frac{K_{2}^{2}}{K_{1}^{2}+K_{2}t}
for all
t
.

Finally, if
C_{\ell}=B_{\ell}
,
C_{r}=B_{r}
, and
h(t)=\frac{K_{2}^{2}}{K_{1}+K_{2}t}
for all
t
, then
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y})
is the
